========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I mean, not in a terribly extreme way, I don’t think. But I do notice I get pulled off track more than I’d like.

I’ll sit down intending to finish a plate for a contract, and then something small will catch me—a bird at the window, or a hardware catalog sitting there, or some half-formed idea for a workshop—and suddenly I’m completely somewhere else. Then I look back and realize I haven’t done what I sat down to do.

I suppose that counts as thinking about things repeatedly, or maybe just… not staying with one thing very well?

I thought it might be good to talk to someone before I let it get worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about your situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety exactly… maybe more like pressure, if that makes sense. I do feel a bit wound up when I look at everything I’ve agreed to, but I’ve been trying to keep it manageable. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your projects and deadlines repeatedly, even when you're trying to focus on other things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not in a terribly extreme way, I don’t think. But I do notice I get pulled off track more than I’d like.\n\nI’ll sit down intending to finish a plate for a contract, and then something small will catch me—a bird at the window, or a hardware catalog sitting there, or some half-formed idea for a workshop—and suddenly I’m completely somewhere else. Then I look back and realize I haven’t done what I sat down to do.\n\nI suppose that counts as thinking about things repeatedly, or maybe just… not staying with one thing very well?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a particular routine?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I like things to be organized in the studio, and I have my preferred way of setting up before I work, but I don’t think it’s a rigid routine or anything I have to do or something bad will happen.\n\nI suppose I can get particular about materials being where I expect them, but that’s more practical than… compulsive, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any tasks or activities because they feel overwhelming?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly avoiding them. I might put something off for a bit if it feels like a lot to get started, but I wouldn’t say I’m refusing to do things or anything like that.\n\nMostly I try to keep moving, even if it’s in smaller pieces. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ll double-check that I’ve locked up the studio or turned something off, but I think that’s pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel driven to do it over and over, or like I can’t stop myself. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to function in your daily life or maintain your relationships due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I hate saying it that way because I’m usually very capable.\n\nMy illustration deadlines have slipped, and a couple of colleagues have had to check in on me more than they normally would. I’ve been telling myself it’s just a busy patch, but… I did overcommit, and I spent money I really needed to keep the studio secure. So now the mortgage is more of a worry than I’d like to admit.\n\nI’m not falling apart, I don’t think, but it is affecting things. I’ve been telling myself it’s just a busy patch, but… I did overcommit, and I spent money I really needed to keep the studio secure. So now the mortgage is more of a worry than I’d like to admit.\n\nI’m not falling apart, I don’t think, but it is affecting things. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when they are going well?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I’m a responsible person, so I do think ahead and I don’t like leaving things loose, but when things are genuinely going well I can usually let myself feel that.\n\nMaybe I get a little cautious, like waiting for the other shoe to drop sometimes, but… I don’t think I’m constantly worrying. I thought it might be good to talk to someone before I let it get worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about your situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety exactly… maybe more like pressure, if that makes sense. But I do notice I get pulled off track more than I’d like.\n\nI’ll sit down intending to finish a plate for a contract, and then something small will catch me—a bird at the window, or a hardware catalog sitting there, or some half-formed idea for a workshop—and suddenly I’m completely somewhere else. Then I look back and realize I haven’t done what I sat down to do.\n\nI suppose that counts as thinking about things repeatedly, or maybe just… not staying with one thing very well?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a particular routine?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any tasks or activities because they feel overwhelming?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly avoiding them. I might put something off for a bit if it feels like a lot to get started, but I wouldn’t say I’m refusing to do things or anything like that.\n\nMostly I try to keep moving, even if it’s in smaller pieces. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I’m trying to keep it proportionate. Is that… the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any tasks or situations that you've been avoiding lately because they make you feel uncomfortable or anxious?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really avoiding in a deliberate way.\n\nThere are things I procrastinate on, like paperwork or emails when I know they might be awkward, but I think that’s fairly normal. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to function in your daily life or maintain your relationships due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I hate saying it that way because I’m usually very capable.\n\nMy illustration deadlines have slipped, and a couple of colleagues have had to check in on me more than they normally would. I’ve been telling myself it’s just a busy patch, but… I did overcommit, and I spent money I really needed to keep the studio secure. I thought it might be good to talk to someone before I let it get worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about your situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety exactly… maybe more like pressure, if that makes sense. I do feel a bit wound up when I look at everything I’ve agreed to, but I’ve been trying to keep it manageable. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your projects and deadlines repeatedly, even when you're trying to focus on other things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not in a terribly extreme way, I don’t think. But I do notice I get pulled off track more than I’d like.\n\nI’ll sit down intending to finish a plate for a contract, and then something small will catch me—a bird at the window, or a hardware catalog sitting there, or some half-formed idea for a workshop—and suddenly I’m completely somewhere else. Then I look back and realize I haven’t done what I sat down to do.\n\nI suppose that counts as thinking about things repeatedly, or maybe just… not staying with one thing very well?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a particular routine?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I like things to be organized in the studio, and I have my preferred way of setting up before I work, but I don’t think it’s a rigid routine or anything I have to do or something bad will happen.\n\nI suppose I can get particular about materials being where I expect them, but that’s more practical than… compulsive, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any tasks or activities because they feel overwhelming?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly avoiding them. I might put something off for a bit if it feels like a lot to get started, but I wouldn’t say I’m refusing to do things or anything like that.\n\nMostly I try to keep moving, even if it’s in smaller pieces. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ll double-check that I’ve locked up the studio or turned something off, but I think that’s pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel driven to do it over and over, or like I can’t stop myself. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to function in your daily life or maintain your relationships due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I hate saying it that way because I’m usually very capable.\n\nMy illustration deadlines have slipped, and a couple of colleagues have had to check in on me more than they normally would. I’ve been telling myself it’s just a busy patch, but… I did overcommit, and I spent money I really needed to keep the studio secure. So now the mortgage is more of a worry than I’d like to admit.\n\nI’m not falling apart, I don’t think, but it is affecting things. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when they are going well?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I’m a responsible person, so I do think ahead and I don’t like leaving things loose, but when things are genuinely going well I can usually let myself feel that.\n\nMaybe I get a little cautious, like waiting for the other shoe to drop sometimes, but… I don’t think I’m constantly worrying. Does that sound within normal limits?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: Not really… not in the sense of something intrusive that I can’t shake.\n\nI do get stuck thinking about work or whether I’ve handled things properly, but I’ve always been conscientious, so I don’t know that I’d call it unusual. If you mean thoughts that feel unwanted or keep looping no matter what I do, I don’t think so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much distress are you experiencing due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: It’s… mild, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. I’m still getting through the day.\n\nBut I am distressed about it. My deadlines slipping bothers me, and I feel embarrassed that colleagues have had to check in on me. The bigger worry is the money — I overcommitted and spent what I really should have kept aside for the studio, so now the mortgage feels… less secure than it should.\n\nSo, yes, it’s upsetting, but I’m trying not to let it take over. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your worry about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say unable, exactly. I mean, it’s there, and it comes up when I’m looking at the bills or thinking about the deadlines, but I can usually redirect myself after a bit.\n\nIt’s not like I’m worrying every minute, at least I don’t think so. I’m trying to keep it proportionate. Is that… the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any tasks or situations that you've been avoiding lately because they make you feel uncomfortable or anxious?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really avoiding in a deliberate way.\n\nThere are things I procrastinate on, like paperwork or emails when I know they might be awkward, but I think that’s fairly normal. I wouldn’t say I’m staying away from situations because I’m anxious. At least, nothing obvious comes to mind. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things for a long time, even after you've dealt with them?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, once something is handled, I usually feel some relief and try to move on.\n\nI might replay it a little if I think I handled it badly, but I don’t think I stay worried for a long time afterward. At least, not in a way that feels unusual to me. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things for no apparent reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not for no reason. Usually if I’m worried, I can point to something—money, work, something I need to finish.\n\nI mean, maybe sometimes there’s a general uneasy feeling, but I wouldn’t say I’m just worrying out of nowhere all the time. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I don’t think so… I mean, I have normal concerns like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’m worrying every day or that it takes over.\n\nMaybe I notice it more when there’s something specific going on, but otherwise I can usually set it aside. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worrying more than you would like to, even when there's no obvious reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’d prefer not to worry at all, obviously, but it doesn’t feel like I’m worrying more than I should, especially not when there isn’t a clear reason.\n\nIf something’s going on, I might think about it, but otherwise I’m usually okay. I hope that’s helpful.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

I thought it might be good to talk to someone before I let it get worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about your situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety exactly… maybe more like pressure, if that makes sense. I do feel a bit wound up when I look at everything I’ve agreed to, but I’ve been trying to keep it manageable. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about your projects and deadlines repeatedly, even when you're trying to focus on other things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not in a terribly extreme way, I don’t think. But I do notice I get pulled off track more than I’d like.\n\nI’ll sit down intending to finish a plate for a contract, and then something small will catch me—a bird at the window, or a hardware catalog sitting there, or some half-formed idea for a workshop—and suddenly I’m completely somewhere else. Then I look back and realize I haven’t done what I sat down to do.\n\nI suppose that counts as thinking about things repeatedly, or maybe just… not staying with one thing very well?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a specific way or follow a particular routine?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I like things to be organized in the studio, and I have my preferred way of setting up before I work, but I don’t think it’s a rigid routine or anything I have to do or something bad will happen.\n\nI suppose I can get particular about materials being where I expect them, but that’s more practical than… compulsive, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any tasks or activities because they feel overwhelming?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not exactly avoiding them. I might put something off for a bit if it feels like a lot to get started, but I wouldn’t say I’m refusing to do things or anything like that.\n\nMostly I try to keep moving, even if it’s in smaller pieces. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ll double-check that I’ve locked up the studio or turned something off, but I think that’s pretty normal, isn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel driven to do it over and over, or like I can’t stop myself. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to function in your daily life or maintain your relationships due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I hate saying it that way because I’m usually very capable.\n\nMy illustration deadlines have slipped, and a couple of colleagues have had to check in on me more than they normally would. I’ve been telling myself it’s just a busy patch, but… I did overcommit, and I spent money I really needed to keep the studio secure. So now the mortgage is more of a worry than I’d like to admit.\n\nI’m not falling apart, I don’t think, but it is affecting things. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when they are going well?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I’m a responsible person, so I do think ahead and I don’t like leaving things loose, but when things are genuinely going well I can usually let myself feel that.\n\nMaybe I get a little cautious, like waiting for the other shoe to drop sometimes, but… I don’t think I’m constantly worrying. Does that sound within normal limits?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: Not really… not in the sense of something intrusive that I can’t shake.\n\nI do get stuck thinking about work or whether I’ve handled things properly, but I’ve always been conscientious, so I don’t know that I’d call it unusual. If you mean thoughts that feel unwanted or keep looping no matter what I do, I don’t think so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much distress are you experiencing due to your current situation?\n\nPatient: It’s… mild, I think. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. I’m still getting through the day.\n\nBut I am distressed about it. My deadlines slipping bothers me, and I feel embarrassed that colleagues have had to check in on me. The bigger worry is the money — I overcommitted and spent what I really should have kept aside for the studio, so now the mortgage feels… less secure than it should.\n\nSo, yes, it’s upsetting, but I’m trying not to let it take over. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your worry about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say unable, exactly. I mean, it’s there, and it comes up when I’m looking at the bills or thinking about the deadlines, but I can usually redirect myself after a bit.\n\nIt’s not like I’m worrying every minute, at least I don’t think so. I’m trying to keep it proportionate. Is that… the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any tasks or situations that you've been avoiding lately because they make you feel uncomfortable or anxious?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really avoiding in a deliberate way.\n\nThere are things I procrastinate on, like paperwork or emails when I know they might be awkward, but I think that’s fairly normal. I wouldn’t say I’m staying away from situations because I’m anxious. At least, nothing obvious comes to mind. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things for a long time, even after you've dealt with them?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, once something is handled, I usually feel some relief and try to move on.\n\nI might replay it a little if I think I handled it badly, but I don’t think I stay worried for a long time afterward. At least, not in a way that feels unusual to me. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things for no apparent reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not for no reason. Usually if I’m worried, I can point to something—money, work, something I need to finish.\n\nI mean, maybe sometimes there’s a general uneasy feeling, but I wouldn’t say I’m just worrying out of nowhere all the time. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I don’t think so… I mean, I have normal concerns like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’m worrying every day or that it takes over.\n\nMaybe I notice it more when there’s something specific going on, but otherwise I can usually set it aside. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worrying more than you would like to, even when there's no obvious reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’d prefer not to worry at all, obviously, but it doesn’t feel like I’m worrying more than I should, especially not when there isn’t a clear reason.\n\nIf something’s going on, I might think about it, but otherwise I’m usually okay. I hope that’s helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worry about your situation is persistent and interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s persistent in that way, no. I mean, I do think about my situation, of course, but I wouldn’t say it’s really interfering with my day-to-day life.\n\nI can still get through what I need to do. Maybe it’s on my mind sometimes, but not constantly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t think so… I mean, I have normal concerns like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’m worrying every day or that it takes over.\n\nMaybe I notice it more when there’s something specific going on, but otherwise I can usually set it aside. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worrying more than you would like to, even when there's no obvious reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I hope that’s helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worry about your situation is persistent and interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s persistent in that way, no. Maybe it’s on my mind sometimes, but not constantly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are excessive and harder to control than you would like?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call them excessive, no. If that makes sense? I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worrying about your situation all the time, even when you're not actively thinking about it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not all the time.
